ParlaMint-ES_2019-02-27-CD190227.u341	No writing. [[Laughter]] Thank you, President. I remind you that in Spain from 2013 to 2017 irregular arrivals of migrants by sea and land increased by 301,2 % - it should be controlled -. While citizens have the right to know what is being done, they also have the right to know what is not being done. You will understand that the state of public security that we find when we arrive at the Government was a lack of resources, lack of staff and lack of coordination, but I am going to answer you about what this Government is doing. Over the past eight months, since the Socialist Party came to the government, irregular immigration and the fight against illegal human trafficking has been one of the priorities of this ministry and of this minister. Migration is one of the major global challenges that generates positive effects on countries and societies when it is legal, secure and orderly. Therefore, combating irregular immigration, trafficking in persons and trafficking in human beings is my main responsibility. Irregular migration generates imbalances and rights violations, both in the societies of countries of origin and transit and in those of destination. Unlike other illicit trafficking and other cross-border crimes, in the case of irregular immigration, the mafia traffics with people, many of them vulnerable as women and minors. Ladies and gentlemen, in order to better understand the reality, I advise you to start with the origin. The causes that lead many young people to leave their countries of origin are much more powerful than those that we hypothetically believe to generate an attraction or, in other words, on the neighbouring African continent the fledgling effect is the main cause of migratory movements. They flee from hunger, flee from injustice, flee out of fear, seeking an opportunity. They flee deceived and flee from extreme needs. If we want to find reasons between ourselves, let us look at the European Union. The European Union generates attraction. The difference in per capita GDP between the two shores of the Mediterranean is the main reason for the decision to emigrate and this makes immigration a structural phenomenon of this time. We are not facing a new reality or a new trend. As you know, Spain, due to its proximity to the main African countries of origin of immigration to Europe, was already the gateway to the European Union in 2006, in particular, 31,678 people who arrived mainly in the Canary Islands. Then we were able together to lay the foundations for migration policy. By calling for foreign policy measures, legal migration and the fight against irregular immigration, we were able to curb the arrivals of cayucos. Thus, we were able to avoid many deaths in the Atlantic and build a relationship of trust and cooperation with our southern neighbours. That is what it is about, to prevent through cooperation and trust. The situation in 2006 is not that of today. Between the two dates, the European Union has faced a refugee crisis such as that of 2015. Africa has only grown; moreover, the forecasts point to greater population growth. In turn, the European acquis has changed in the area of asylum and borders. It is no longer the same as in 2006. However, despite the much work that has been done in the European Union, we have not yet achieved a common immigration policy, but we must face up to current trends and pressures and be respectful of the principles of the European Union, Articles 2 and 6 of the Treaty on Union and Article 80 of the Treaty on the Functioning, Responsibility and Solidarity of the countries of the Union. In the case of Spain, as I told you, since the end of 2014 the number of arrivals of irregular immigrants by sea has grown. This trend has been particularly worrying since mid-2017, when changes in the deployment of surveillance in the Central Mediterranean and Italian unilateral decisions led to the displacement to the Western Mediterranean. Ladies and gentlemen, this is the situation that we are in June 2018, when I assumed the portfolio of the Interior, and since then we have been dedicated to fighting illegal immigration, having recovered the immigration policy after the last years of stagnation. With the Socialist Government, migration policy comes again. We wanted more cooperation and more confidence. The priority objective is obviously prevention in the first place, first of all by addressing the real reasons for this runaway effect to which I have referred. In this context, there is the area of security and we can be useful in avoiding the action of human trafficking organizations and preventing the risk of endangering the lives of migrants. This prevention work goes far beyond repression, because it is the one that really saves all lives, so it can be said that the action of the Interior fighting against irregular immigration prevents suffering and violation of human rights. This is why we have worked these months to strengthen cooperation with third countries, which is a key and cross-cutting element in migration policy. Both the Secretary of State and I have developed an intense international agenda. We have been visited by more than 20 foreign authorities and have made 17 international trips. We have travelled to Morocco - five times I have met with its Minister of the Interior - Algeria, Senegal, Mauritania, Guinea-Conakry, the Gambia, Côte d'Ivoire and Ghana. It was the first time that a Minister of the Interior of the Kingdom of Spain had visited these last four countries and participated in cooperation. In all cases we have met with presidents, prime ministers and vice-presidents eager to cooperate and intensify cooperation for the prevention of illegal immigration. The President of Guinea-Conakry, for example, Alpha Condé, who has also been President of the African Union, told me that the problem of irregular immigration is comparable to a new modern form of slavery. Africa does not want these irregular immigration routes for its young people, Africa wants its young people for the future of the continent, but the means they have must be strengthened by the European Union. I would like to convince you of the need to develop a diverse and cross-cutting comprehensive cooperation that will help to control migratory flows more effectively. With these trips I have sought to strengthen bilateral cooperation on migration by identifying common interests, building trust and collaborating more and more in the fight against trafficking in human beings. We have also cooperated in civil protection and other matters of interest to them. I also stress that, since June, we have signed international conventions to combat crime with four countries in sub-Saharan Africa and the Maghreb, such as Morocco. At the bilateral level, we have also done so. Joint work between the National Police and the Civil Guard and the security forces of third countries, such as joint teams in Mauritania, Senegal, the GAR-SI Sahel, has allowed the exchange of information, as well as the arrest and dismantling of important illegal immigration networks, preventing irregular migrants from leaving our shores. I also highlight the work carried out at our borders, with greater protection and security, and the fight against trafficking in human beings and trafficking in persons carried out by the State Security Forces and Bodies. Work has also been carried out on the disarticulation of networks, with different operations; a national strategy against organized crime, where irregular migration is one of the bases, which has been re-enforced, also forming part of the Intelligence Directive, which had disappeared for you. We hope, as reflected in that strategy, that we will soon adopt a national strategic plan against trafficking in human beings. We're working on the return. We are returning irregular immigrants in a much larger number than you did. In addition, we have strengthened our capacities for coordination, border control and care and assistance to rescued persons. In early August 2008, we created the Coordinating Authority to deal with irregular immigration in the Gibraltar area and the Alboran Sea. For the comprehensive approach we want, ladies and gentlemen, coherence and coordination are vital. At the domestic level, we are doing our utmost to deal more effectively with migratory pressure, which was not done between 2014 and 2017. We have set out to guarantee security with humanity, and that is where we stand. I am not going to lose ten seconds on this issue because it is a notorious thing how the Asylum and Refugee Office is now formed, and Acnur and CEAR can say so. You said that from 2016 to 2018 arrivals multiplied by seven. Why didn't you say that from 2016 to 2017 you multiplied the arrivals by four, which is when you were and didn't do anything? He gave this data because the other one wasn't the one he really liked. That is why I say to you that when you talk about these issues you have to be a little careful on those extremes. We will continue to pursue this policy of coordination and cooperation with the countries of origin. We have greatly increased economic aid and development cooperation with the countries I have indicated, and we are also implementing joint research teams with all of them. Within the framework of the European Union, we have finally managed to get Morocco to receive the assistance and cooperation that it has not received in recent years, such as a transfer of EUR 140 million, so that they can invest in the border control of their country and in the training of personnel, in which we are working. Detentions have been increased in a significant way in the Mafia field. That is why we want cooperation and we want to continue to pursue a migration policy. I have told you again that the police and the Civil Guard like and want help, data, to be given financial, material and personal means. On both the borders of Ceuta and Melilla, European borders, this aid has been implemented, and I have already told you how it will be carried out. You also referred to the falsehood that the jumps to the fence had increased. That's not true. The most important entrances by leaps to the fence you have both in Ceuta and Melilla during the governments of the People's Party. They're objective facts. By Ceuta and Melilla in recent months irregular entrances are being reduced. We are participating in the intervention and cooperation with the various ministries to help all the collectives, all the NGOs that assist migrants, but I say again that we are in favour of a safe, legal and orderly migration policy, and I have just indicated to you the results of February. This is the last question of the legislature and I would like to take advantage of this space to thank the comrades of the Ministry of the Interior for the vocation of public service, their work and their commitment. I thank the Socialist Group for its cooperation and work. Ladies and gentlemen, I always work in handling data that are public, that are part of our democratic system and that sustains the reality of Spain. Listening and the debate, as I have always said, enriches. Parliamentary life has taught me that I am a politician - now I can tell you - who believes in this government's project, who believes in cooperation and who believes in prosperous countries. Do not doubt one thing, if we go to the opposition, we will cooperate, because the Socialist Party is a state party, today the President of the Government reminded us, we are where we are a state party. But as I have indicated before, that game with falsehoods, that obviating reality, blurring public reality, and I hope that Spanish society will decide that we will continue and that we can continue with these social policies and with policies of understanding, in my case of security and human rights. It will be the Spanish society - it would be missing more! - that will have to analyze and examine us all. Since we are talking about emigration, let me finish with a verse from a Somali poet, Warsan Shire, who says: no one climbs their children to a boat unless the water is safer than the earth. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2019-02-27-CD190227.u347	Finally, we moved on to the debate on the report on the implementation of the House's budget for the 2017 financial year, in accordance with Rule 31.1.2 of the Rules of Procedure. For the presentation of a summary of the report, I give the floor to the first secretary of the Bureau.
ParlaMint-ES_2019-02-27-CD190227.u348	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, in accordance with the principle of budgetary autonomy enshrined in Article 72.1 of the Constitution and in compliance with Article 31.1.2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Congress of Deputies, the report on the implementation of the House's budget for 2017, the general outlines of which are set out below, is presented to the plenary. The fiscal year of the Congress of Deputies for 2017 began in application of Article 134.4 of the Constitution under a situation of extension of the General Budgets of the State for 2016. The Bureau of the Congress of Deputies, at its meeting of 28 March 2017, approved the draft budgets of the House for 2017, which was integrated into the General Budgets of the State for that year approved by Law 3/2017 of 27 June, with an overall amount of 85,517,350 euros, which represents 0.03% of the General Budgets of the State. Of the total amount of appropriations initially authorised, those actually committed amounted to EUR 77 254 741.09, representing an implementation of 90.34 % of the budget initially approved, bringing the uncommitted balance to EUR 8 262 608.91, representing 9.66 % of the budget. Of the total appropriations committed in the financial year 2017, EUR 71,327 774.75 were paid during the same obligations and during 2018 payments amounting to EUR 5,942,626.51 were made, according to the following breakdown: outstanding payments at 31 December 2017 arising from obligations for the financial year and made between 1 January and 31 March 2018, EUR 5,942,626.51. Second, cash balance at the end of 2017, EUR 15 660.17. In addition, several credit transfers, approved by the Bureau of the House at its meetings on 21 March and 17 October 2017, for a total of €933,000, have been requested during the 2017 financial year. Congress Hall of Deputies, February 27, 2019. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-ES_2019-02-27-CD190227.u349	Thank you very much, Madam First Secretary. As there were no interventions by the groups, the meeting was suspended until 9 a.m. tomorrow. It was seven and thirty-five minutes in the afternoon.
